## 2026-11-11 — M37 implemented: BETA26 Vis/Flux drain semantics (native GameTest pending)

- Fresh source comparison: pinned BETA26 `AuraHandler.java` at [954022bb](https://github.com/TheDarkTower314/Thaumcraft-6-Source-Code/blob/954022bb777b7546281fb36df8522f0ba6b43f81/src/main/java/thaumcraft/common/world/aura/AuraHandler.java). Both `drainVis` and `drainFlux` cap positive requests to the current amount, return the capped amount for simulation without mutation, and apply the real debit through the respective modify method. The original source does **not** reject finite negative requests: a negative request returns a negative value and increases the stored resource. This oddity is now explicitly covered rather than silently normalized.
- Production discrepancy fixed: `ThaumcraftAuraSavedData.drainFlux` previously rejected all `amount <= 0`, unlike BETA26 and unlike the already source-compatible `drainVis`. It now rejects only non-finite floats; finite negative Flux requests preserve the original negative-return / resource-increase behavior. Non-finite values remain intentionally rejected as a port safety guard to prevent NaN/Infinity from entering persistent aura state; that is a deliberate hardening, not a claim of byte-for-byte legacy behavior.
- Added native GameTest assertions on an isolated second aura chunk: Vis/Flux simulation, exact partial debit, capped simulation, capped real debit to zero, exhausted no-op, finite negative source behavior, NaN/±Infinity rejection, missing-chunk zero/no-create, and Base preservation. The two-process restart runner now requires every M37 acceptance marker in the write phase; read phase remains isolated and unchanged.
- Changed files: `ThaumcraftAuraSavedData.java`, `AuraGenerationGameTests.java`, `tools/run_crystal_spread_gametest.sh`, this lane journal. No shared registry, Gradle, workflow YAML, main, or foreign feature branch changed. PR #1/#2/#3 changed-path lists were checked; none overlaps these M37 paths. The known PR #4/#3 `SalisMundusItem.java` overlap remains untouched.

The port's immutable record updates previously reproduced the lower bound but missed this upper bound on a finite negative drain (which is intentionally preserved from BETA26). Added the same ceiling to production Vis/Flux drain mutations and extended the GameTest with simulated and real -40000 requests: simulation returns -40000 without mutation; real mutation returns -40000 but stores exactly 32766 in both channels; Base remains 100. Non-finite guard assertions now verify no mutation at the saturated value.

## 2026-11-11 — M37 GameTest oracle corrected: simulated negative over-cap drain

- Fresh main baseline was re-read: `main` HEAD `a4280834adc5a20b6951098af937d30ca4aeca56`; custom lane PR #4 HEAD before this fix was `dfdb0e282c2ef728b425938ed4e711af27ddab05` (474 commits ahead, 0 behind). - Exact BETA26 source was fetched from pinned upstream commit [954022bb](https://github.com/TheDarkTower314/Thaumcraft-6-Source-Code/tree/954022bb777b7546281fb36df8522f0ba6b43f81): `AuraHandler.drainVis/drainFlux` clamps only requests greater than current availability, then calls the modify method with `-amount` and `doit=!simulate`; `AuraChunk.setVis/setFlux` clamps stored values to [0,32766]. Therefore a simulated request of `-40000` must return `-40000` while preserving the pre-call value, and only the subsequent non-simulated request may raise the stored value to 32766.
- The previous exact-head native GameTest [#42958546337](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/42958546337) **FAILED** after the earlier M37 assertions passed. Its actual server log shows the test first wrote Vis/Flux from 0 to 2 via a real `-2` request, then simulated `-40000`. Production correctly returned the negative request without mutation, leaving both values at 2. The test incorrectly expected 32766 **before** the real `-40000` call, so the assertion oracle was wrong; this was not evidence of a production drain defect. The log contains `TC_GAMETEST_AURA_DRAIN_SIMULATE`, `...PARTIAL`, `...CAPPED`, and `...NEGATIVE_BETA26` PASS markers before this assertion.
- Corrected `AuraGenerationGameTests.java`: the simulated negative over-cap assertion now expects the pre-write values 2/2 and emits a dedicated `TC_GAMETEST_AURA_DRAIN_NEGATIVE_CAP_SIMULATE=PASS` marker; the subsequent real negative over-cap assertion remains responsible for expecting 32766/32766. The non-finite guard now correctly expects the already-saturated 32766/32766 state to remain unchanged.
- Updated `tools/run_crystal_spread_gametest.sh` to require the new simulation-preservation marker, so the two-process runner cannot accept the write phase without this exact no-mutation assertion.
- Changed files in this correction: `src/neoforge/java/dev/ehtid/thaumcraft/world/AuraGenerationGameTests.java`, `tools/run_crystal_spread_gametest.sh`, and this lane journal. Production `ThaumcraftAuraSavedData.java` was not changed because the failing log and pinned source agree on its behavior. PR #1/#2/#3 changed-file lists were checked; neither the M37 test, runner, nor journal paths overlap those PRs. The existing PR #4/#3 overlap in `SalisMundusItem.java` is unrelated and untouched.
- Matching full NeoForge CI [#42958546370](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/42958546370) **SUCCESS** on the previous code HEAD `dfdb0e2`: Core parity, NeoForge build, dedicated-server smoke and JAR packaging. This does not make M37 pass; the native GameTest above failed. No exact-head CI result exists yet for the corrected test/runner.
- Correction commits: test [62b35e9e502e4b3c2754f4dae8a3844925ffb01d](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/commit/62b35e9e502e4b3c2754f4dae8a3844925ffb01d); runner [a5e13a6d47f929687acad7f0e2d11304eb0c0a06](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/commit/a5e13a6d47f929687acad7f0e2d11304eb0c0a06). Deterministic RandomSource seeds force the original nextInt(3+generation)==0 tick gate. ### M41 diagnosis and fixture correction — 2026-11-11 (exact-head client run #43002919350)

- Inspected the failed real-client artifact [#13849088133](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/43002919350/artifacts/13849088133) and decoded job log [#43002919350](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/43002919350). The jar break portion is genuinely successful: `JAR_REAL_BLOCK_BREAK=PASS`; captured client PCM matches original BETA26 `jar3.ogg` at correlation 0.9633 (baseline 0.0688, pitch factor 0.8000). The job then fails at `JAR_LABEL_WRONG_SIDE_MUTATED=YES`; it never reached the negative PAGE matcher or the correct-side removal/drop assertion. This is not an audio matcher failure.
- The cause is a reversed fixture orientation, not a demonstrated production-code defect. Original BETA26 `BlockJar.onBlockActivated` checks `side.ordinal() == TileJarFillable.facing`. The fixture explicitly sets `facing=3`, which is `Direction.SOUTH` in the modern port. Minecraft yaw 0 looks south (+Z); yaw 180 looks north (-Z). Before this correction the purported wrong-side player stood at z=252.5 facing north, so the ray hit the configured SOUTH face and correctly removed the label. The purported correct-side player stood at z=249.0 facing south, so the ray hit NORTH. The test's two cases were inverted.
- Production `EssentiaJarBlock.useWithoutItem` was left unchanged. Corrected only `tools/client_jar_sound_smoke.py`: the negative case now stands north at z=249.5 and looks south (NORTH face); the positive case stands south at z=253.5 and looks north (SOUTH face). The positive position remains 2 horizontal blocks from the drop and 2.5 blocks in 3D from the item entity, outside the 1-block pickup radius but within the 5-block interaction reach and the test's 3-block item-entity query.
- Strengthened `tools/verify_jar_label_sound.py` to require both exact position/yaw pairs, `facing=3b`'s NORTH negative / SOUTH positive markers, so this orientation inversion cannot silently recur.
- Open-PR path comparison was refreshed against #1/#2/#3: neither `tools/client_jar_sound_smoke.py` nor `tools/verify_jar_label_sound.py` overlaps their current changed-file sets. No production registry, Gradle, global localization, M07 screen, or other lane was changed.
- Commits: [8ebae42](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/commit/8ebae4206842065cfa8c37c19b604a5c2f526e6f) (smoke geometry), [1058cfb](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/commit/1058cfbca13aa7c365e69f399d6d6492ad7780d4) (static geometry guard). Both are on `feature/closure-near-complete`; PR #4 remains Draft/Open/Unmerged.
- On the previous exact head `883da929`, full NeoForge CI [#43002917833](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/43002917833) and aura/crystal GameTests [#43002917864](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/43002917864) completed SUCCESS. These do not validate the new smoke fixture. The M41 client test failed as described.
- Acceptance is still pending. Require a fresh exact-head client run to pass: wrong NORTH face preserves filter and produces no PAGE match; SOUTH face removes filter, drops a blank label and matches original page1/page2 PCM. Require a full NeoForge build/JAR/dedicated-server CI on the same code head. Fixed audit remains 59/100; M16 remains 4/6; no points are awarded for this test-only correction.

The same run accepted M42 emptying and all four M43 SoundType events: Place `jar4.ogg` 0.9979, Hit `jar1.ogg` 0.6227, Step `jar4.ogg` 0.9999, Fall `jar3.ogg` 0.3776. This closes the previous M41 PAGE/PCM blocker on that exact HEAD; it does not close M16 as a whole.

#### Nitor flame RGB source mismatch (new code)
- Pinned BETA26 `TileNitor.update` passes `state.getBlock().getMapColor(...).colorValue` into `FXDispatcher.drawNitorFlames`; `BlockNitor.getMapColor` uses `MapColor.getBlockColor(dye)`. The port instead built `DustParticleOptions` from `LegacyNitorColors.Variant.particleRgb`, which is the modern dye/firework RGB palette (e.g. yellow `0xFED83D`), not the block MapColor. The modern registry already assigns each Nitor block `DyeColor.getMapColor()`, so the particle now uses that same map-color RGB.
- `NitorBlock` maps each of the 16 legacy dye RGB identifiers through its `LegacyNitorColors.Variant.modernDye` to `DyeColor.getMapColor().col`, caches the resulting `DustParticleOptions`, and emits an opt-in runtime marker with dye/map/particle RGB. Unknown legacy variant colors fail fast rather than silently using the wrong shade. The codec's one-argument constructor uses the first valid legacy variant (white), not an unmapped `0xFFFFFF` sentinel.
- The smoke verifier now requires exactly one 20/20/2 client-tick marker and exactly one color marker; it rejects mismatched particle/map RGB, reuse of the dye RGB, missing evidence, and out-of-range values. The regression script checks all 16 legacy variant identifiers and exercises positive/negative verifier fixtures.

- [`74d4867`](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/commit/74d486760c6721dea9038d49d2914fdb21a61538) — require runtime map-color evidence in verifier.
- [`8c898fa`](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/commit/8c898fa644dd103328f2fc76da19793570b63768) — positive/negative verifier fixtures and 16-variant source contract.

- [`41bba45`](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/commit/41bba45e80fa2d3abe1ae05ce7727e37b8d6c225) — preserve a valid white fallback for the block codec constructor.

## 2026-11-12 — Nitor first-render age: correct the real 1.21 particle-engine ordering

- Fresh main HEAD remains `a4280834adc5a20b6951098af937d30ca4aeca56`; branch HEAD before this fix was `f09dae55a3258ad53a907260b568a8ca18b4041f`. PR #4 remains Open/Draft/Unmerged. The actual client log recorded `TC_SMOKE_NITOR_CORE_RENDERED=YES id=1 frame=457 age=0` and `TC_SMOKE_NITOR_FLAME_RENDERED=YES id=1 frame=264 age=0`. The first post-update scale markers then appeared at age 1. Artifact [#13931703556](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/43193761422/artifacts/13931703556) contains the real before/active screenshots and full client log.
- This falsifies the previous `b8b1cce` assumption that the modern particle engine advances a newly queued particle before its first render. In this actual 1.21.1 client, the first render occurs at the constructor age. BETA26 `FXGeneric.onUpdate` increments age before its first render, so the port must initialize at age 1, not 0. The previous age-0 change was a production parity regression, not merely a verifier mismatch.
- Restored `this.age = 1` in `NitorParticleClientEvents.java`. Moved the first-scale smoke marker from `tick()` to `markRendered()`, so the marker now reports the scale of the actual first visible frame rather than the first post-update sample. The existing verifier already requires first rendered core frame 457/age 1 and flame frame 265/age 1; it now receives evidence from the correct render point.
- Updated `tools/test_nitor_particle.py` to model the observed render-before-first-tick ordering, require the source age 1, and reject synthetic age-0 first-render logs for both particles.
- Commits: regression contract [`0f1bc78`](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/commit/0f1bc782870b7fa49f4a3f3ac7290bfac50dfba2); production age/render marker [`dee6f75`](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/commit/dee6f758241f417629300275bd88bfd006698ff9).
- No resource, registry, Gradle, shared smoke bootstrap, global localization, main, or foreign feature branch changed. ## 2026-11-12 — Nitor follow-up: correct the observed two-tick pre-render queue

- Re-read the exact-head run [#43206767450](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/43206767450), job log and uploaded client artifact [#13939082588](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/43206767450/artifacts/13939082588). The run **failed in the real Minecraft client**: the first visible core frame was ('457', '3'). The actual log records both first visible quads at age 3 (core frame 457, flame frame 267), not age 1. The earlier age-1 success was on older HEAD 7b04d45, not this current b2ca864 head.
- This disproves the previous assumption that the current block-entity emission path renders before its first client tick. On this exact 1.21.1 path, the queued particle is advanced twice before its first visible getU0; the observed age 3 with constructor seed 1 is direct evidence of those two updates.
- BETA26 FXGeneric.onUpdate increments particle age before the first visible render, so its first visible age is 1. - Fresh main HEAD: `89ff94ff46bd5b600b074e99bc08494b33dc1183`. Own branch before this iteration: `89ff94ff46bd5b600b074e99bc08494b33dc1183`; PR [#4](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/pull/4) remains Open/Draft/Unmerged.

## 2026-11-12 — Nitor: stabilize the BETA26 age-one state across queued client ticks

- Fresh main HEAD: `a4280834adc5a20b6951098af937d30ca4aeca56`. Own branch before this fix: `6010752a1c8d33bbecbb6539c9e78e063e125f48`; PR [#4](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/pull/4) remains Open/Draft/Unmerged.
- Inspected exact-head real-client failure [#43264292811](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/43264292811) and downloaded/decoded artifact [#13966062028](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/43264292811/artifacts/13966062028). Its actual `client-smoke/client.log` records the first core quad at frame 457 / age 2 and the first flame quad at frame 266 / age 2. The old age=-1 seed plus a render guard hid negative/zero frames but still allowed extra queued ticks to advance the particle before first render. The real-client acceptance correctly failed.
- Rechecked original BETA26 source `FXGeneric.java` at pinned commit [`954022bb777b7546281fb36df8522f0ba6b43f81`](https://github.com/TheDarkTower314/Thaumcraft-6-Source-Code/blob/954022bb777b7546281fb36df8522f0ba6b43f81/src/main/java/thaumcraft/client/fx/particles/FXGeneric.java): the inherited particle age starts at 0; `onUpdate()` increments age before movement; the first source update therefore renders at age 1 after exactly one movement.
- Production fix: seed modern Nitor particles at age 0. The first client tick now corresponds to the first BETA26 update. If NeoForge ticks the queued particle again before the first render, hold it at age 1, restore the age-one previous-position interpolation origin, and skip further movement/random drift until the first actual render. Once rendered, normal tick cadence resumes. This avoids assuming a fixed two- or three-tick queue delay and preserves the first visible frame, scale, motion and lifetime phase.
- Regression: `tools/test_nitor_particle.py` now models 1–8 queued ticks before first render, asserts exactly one movement update and the original interpolation origin, and requires the actual Java source to hold age 1 until first render. The existing log verifier remains strict: flame frame 265/age 1 and core frame 457/age 1; age 2 still fails.
- Changed files: `src/neoforge/java/dev/ehtid/thaumcraft/client/NitorParticleClientEvents.java`, `tools/test_nitor_particle.py`, and this lane journal. PR #1/#2/#3 changed-file lists have no overlap with these paths; PR #3's existing `SalisMundusItem.java` overlap with PR #4 is untouched. No shared registry, Gradle, workflow, global localization, main, or foreign branch changed.
- Before this fix, exact-head general NeoForge/Core/JAR/dedicated-server CI [#43264292869](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/43264292869) and Aura/Crystal GameTests [#43264293622](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/43264293622) were SUCCESS. The exact-head Nitor/Salis real-client run [#43264292811](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/43264292811) was FAILURE at the age-2 marker. No result for this new fix is claimed until its own CI/client run completes.
- Fixed audit remains **59/100**, M16 **4/6**, M08 **5/9**. No points awarded. Next: inspect the exact-new-head Nitor/Salis client, full NeoForge/JAR/server CI and Aura/Crystal GameTests. If the real first-render markers are age 1 and the other source-derived particle checks pass, resume the RU/EN Arcane Workbench four-cell OCR matrix.

## 12.11.2026 — M16 Arcane Workbench client smoke: generated-shell contamination

**Observed failure:** [GitHub Actions #43287796076](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/43287796076) failed all four EN/RU, GUI scale 2/3 matrix jobs *before Minecraft launched*. In job 131425679579, `tools/test_client_workbench_world.py` passed 44 assertions, but `bash -n client-workbench-gui-generated.sh` failed on line 1: `Workbench GUI OCR matcher: PASS (20 positive/negative assertions)`. The generator executed the OCR self-test with inherited stdout, so its diagnostic text preceded the generated Bash script. This is a confirmed smoke-harness defect, **not evidence of a broken game GUI**.

**Fix:** `tools/client_workbench_gui_smoke.py` routes the OCR self-test's stdout to stderr while retaining `check=True`, leaving stdout exclusively for the generated shell. `tools/test_client_workbench_world.py` now asserts this contract. ## 2026-11-12 — Nitor real-client smoke: distinguish tick frames from GPU samples

- Fresh main HEAD: `a4280834adc5a20b6951098af937d30ca4aeca56`; PR #4 head before this iteration: `2364f50e92dee1ece1d47f3f56f45b935cbff8b2`. PR #4 remains Open/Draft/Unmerged. The current changed paths are lane-owned Nitor particle instrumentation and verifier/tests; PR #1/#2/#3 do not overlap these paths. No merge or foreign-branch write.
- Inspected exact-head Nitor/Salis real-client run [#43298755201](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/43298755201) and downloaded artifact [#13982215891](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/43298755201/artifacts/13982215891). The real client started, the first Nitor core rendered at frame 457/age 1, the first flame rendered at frame 265/age 1, the client ticker reached 20 ticks / 20 flames / 2 cores, and additive blending was 770/1. The GPU-sampled flame frames were 264..270 (7/8); frame 271 was not sampled before the verifier aborted the script, so the subsequent Salis interaction/audio/native-chunk probe never ran. The real screenshot shows the Nitor effect. This is a real-client sampling failure, not evidence that the frame formula or first-visible state is wrong.
- Root cause: the old verifier conflated eight distinct BETA26 update frames with eight frames necessarily sampled by a software-rendered GPU in one short capture window. The same first particle's render-detail markers show ages 1, 5, and 9; the renderer skips intermediate samples while the particle's update age continues.
- Added smoke-only per-particle tick-frame markers for the first flame, emitted only after that particle has actually rendered once. Added `tools/nitor_frame_coverage.py`: it requires the actual first render frame 265, at least two real GPU frame samples, and eight consecutive post-render particle ages mapping exactly to all BETA26 atlas frames 264..271. It does not weaken the atlas sequence or accept an unrendered particle.
- Updated `tools/verify_nitor_client_tick.py` to report rendered samples separately from tick-frame coverage. Updated `tools/test_nitor_client_tick.py` with positive and adversarial fixtures: sparse renderer sampling is accepted only when the same real particle's tick sequence covers all eight frames; missing, duplicated, non-consecutive, or incorrectly mapped frames fail.
- Local isolated helper regression: **PASS** (1 positive + 4 negative cases); the existing real log's 7 sampled frames plus the expected age-2..9 tick sequence pass the new distinction. This is a parser/contract test, not a new Minecraft-client pass. The exact-head full NeoForge/Aura CI and the four-cell Workbench GUI matrix still need their own results; the prior Nitor/Salis run failed before Salis use.
- Changed files: `src/neoforge/java/dev/ehtid/thaumcraft/client/NitorParticleClientEvents.java`, new `tools/nitor_frame_coverage.py`, `tools/verify_nitor_client_tick.py`, `tools/test_nitor_client_tick.py`, and this journal.
- Fixed audit remains **59/100**, M16 **4/6**, M08 **5/9**. No point awarded: this iteration improves the fidelity of real-client acceptance instrumentation; it does not add a gameplay feature or close M16. Next: inspect the exact-new-head Nitor/Salis client run. Require first render age 1, all eight tick frames, at least two actual render samples, original dust PCM correlation, real Salis particle burst, and native chunk-unload/reload cleanup. ## 2026-11-13 — M16 Workbench: synchronize primal requirement mask to the client

- Fresh main remains `a4280834adc5a20b6951098af937d30ca4aeca56`; lane base and current code HEAD before this iteration were `d9cd831cae5212662870ee8814c14c16bf99daa4`. Re-read the canonical roadmap, AGENTS, parallel workflow and fixed audit. PR #4 remains Draft/Open/Unmerged. Current closure-lane paths `ArcaneWorkbenchMenu.java` and `tools/client_workbench_gui_smoke.py` do not overlap the active alchemy/research/infusion/golem/coordination branch diffs; PR #3's separate `SalisMundusItem.java` overlap is unchanged.
- Exact-head real Minecraft Workbench run [#43324170625](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/43324170625), RU/scale 2, showed the GUI really opened and the screenshot visibly contained the localized title, 170 available Vis and 20 Vis cost. OCR and title/panel pixel checks passed, but the new render-path marker was absent. This was not an OCR false-negative: the screen's marker requires `requiredCrystalMask()==63`.
- Root cause in `ArcaneWorkbenchMenu`: the client constructor creates the menu with an empty `SimpleContainer`, then the shared constructor recomputed `crystalMaskSlot(bench)`. That helper returns zero for the client `SimpleContainer`; its DataSlot setter was a no-op, so the server's six-primal mask could never be synchronized. The server-side DataSlot was correct, but the client renderer never received it. This suppressed the BETA26 required-crystal highlights and blocked the strict GUI marker.
- Fixed by making the required-mask DataSlot side-specific like the Vis and cost slots: the client constructor passes `DataSlot.standalone()`; the server constructor passes `crystalMaskSlot(bench)`; the shared constructor stores and registers the supplied slot. Added `tools/test_workbench_crystal_mask_sync.py` and invoked it from the existing client-workbench smoke generator. The real-client marker remains fail-closed and still requires cost 20, sufficient synced Vis and mask 63.
- Changed files: `src/neoforge/java/dev/ehtid/thaumcraft/menu/ArcaneWorkbenchMenu.java`, `tools/client_workbench_gui_smoke.py`, new `tools/test_workbench_crystal_mask_sync.py`, and this journal.
- The previous exact-head NeoForge/Core/JAR/dedicated-server run [#43324170523](https://github.com/craftkot/tgaumcraft/actions/runs/43324170523) is **SUCCESS** on the pre-fix code. It does not validate this change. New compile and four-cell RU/EN × GUI-scale real-client checks must complete on the new HEAD before acceptance. The previous RU/scale-2 screenshot proves the menu rendered but the required mask did not pass the client-side acceptance marker.
- No audit points awarded. Fixed rubric remains **59/100**, M16 **4/6**; this iteration repairs one real client DataSlot synchronization defect but still needs exact-head NeoForge build and all four actual-client jobs. Next: inspect the new compile and matrix; accept only if every cell emits exactly one marker with `crystal_mask=63` and visible primal highlights.
